**Dedication**

**To Gautama, the Supreme Arahant,\
whose liberation revealed the path beyond the world;**

**to the Dhamma that guides the renunciant\
from ethical discipline to concentration,\
from concentration to wisdom,\
and from wisdom to liberation;**

**and to the generations of renunciants\
who have devoted their lives to preserving,\
practising, and transmitting the path.**

May this book serve those who come to the Dhamma\
with sincerity, cultivate the discipline of renunciation,\
and aspire to the supreme liberation of Arahantship.

**May the path to the Deathless continue to be practised\
and made known.**

**\**

**The Intellectual Foundation of My Writings**

The writings of Bhante Mudita Bhikkhu Thera are guided by an Arahanist intellectual foundation, with a singular purpose: to seek, examine, and communicate the truth concerning spiritual existence and Gautama\'s original liberation teaching.

The following principles guide this body of work:

**1. Truth is the objective.**

Truth is the ultimate purpose of intellectual and spiritual inquiry. Our concern is to recognise what is true, examine it carefully, and communicate it faithfully.

**2. Gautama\'s original liberation teaching is central.**

The early **Pāḷi** suttas still constitute the primary textual foundation of this inquiry. The words, terminology, and teachings preserved in these discourses deserve direct and careful examination.

**3. The wider Indian spiritual heritage is essential.**

The inquiry draws upon the three major bodies of Indian spiritual literature: Vedic, Jain, and **Pāḷi** sources. Together, they provide an extensive textual foundation for examining spiritual existence, consciousness, renunciation, and liberation.

**4. Vedic knowledge deserves its proper standing.**

Vedic knowledge, including the teachings concerning Ātman and Brahman, the Munis, and the Upaniṣads, is approached with the seriousness and respect it deserves as an integral part of India\'s spiritual heritage.

**5. Arahanism provides the intellectual standpoint.**

Arahanism is the framework through which these questions are examined, with Gautama\'s liberation teaching at its centre. It is not simply a subdivision of Buddhism or the Śramaṇa tradition, but a distinct commitment to recovering, articulating, and preserving the liberation teaching of Gautama, the Supreme Arahant.

**6. The pursuit of truth takes precedence over the study of religious developments.**

These writings are concerned principally with identifying and communicating truth, rather than tracing the historical evolution of religious doctrines or cataloguing their subsequent additions and transformations.

**7. Textual precision is indispensable.**

Every examination must remain attentive to original terminology, linguistic context, textual evidence, and the precise meaning of the teachings. Conclusions must arise from careful investigation rather than inherited assumptions.

**A Guiding Principle**

**Seek the truth. Examine the evidence. Communicate what is true.**

These principles guide my books, essays, translations, commentaries, and ongoing research. They reflect a sustained commitment to the pursuit of truth and the recovery of Gautama\'s original liberation teaching.

Bhante Mudita

*Ācariya & Spiritual Founder*

Arahanist Order of Canada

**\**

**Acknowledgments**

The preparation of *Vandanā Pūjā: Homage and Veneration* is an offering to the Dhamma and to the enduring aspiration for liberation. This book brings together traditional acts of homage and chanting, ethical commitments, reflections on renunciation, and an introduction to the religious purpose and training of the Arahanist Order of Canada.

My first acknowledgment is to the generations of renunciants who have preserved, studied, practised, and transmitted the teachings contained in the Theravāda Pāli Canon. Their dedication to the Dhamma and the discipline of the renunciant life has made it possible for later generations to encounter a path of practice directed toward liberation from worldly bondage. The preservation of these teachings is not merely an inheritance to be admired; it carries a responsibility to study them carefully, put them into practice, and transmit them with integrity.

I am grateful to the teachers and fellow practitioners whose guidance, example, questions, and discussions have contributed to my understanding of monastic discipline, meditation, and the path of renunciation. The work of spiritual formation requires sustained learning, honest examination, and the willingness to bring one\'s conduct and practice into closer alignment with the Dhamma.

I also acknowledge the people who support the establishment and development of the Arahanist Order of Canada. A living religious community depends upon many forms of service: the preparation of a place for practice, the preservation and sharing of teachings, the organisation of religious activities, material support, and the sincere participation of those who gather to learn and practise. Such contributions help create the conditions in which the Dhamma can be studied, chanted, taught, and lived.

My gratitude extends to all those who approach the Dhamma with sincerity---whether they begin with a few moments of chanting, undertake the Five Precepts, observe the Uposatha Eight Precepts, enter a period of dedicated training, or commit themselves to the full renunciant life. Every genuine step toward ethical integrity, sense restraint, mental cultivation, and liberation deserves to be taken seriously.

This book is offered in the hope that its readers will find in it more than words to recite. May the chants become occasions for recollection; may the precepts become foundations for conduct; may study lead to practice; and may practice deepen the aspiration to abandon worldly attachment and realise the Deathless.

May whatever is wholesome in the preparation and use of this book be dedicated to the preservation of the Dhamma, the flourishing of sincere renunciant practice, and the welfare of all who seek the path to liberation from worldly bondage.

## A Welcome to the Arahanist Order

**A Canadian Theravāda Lineage**

![](media/image4.png){width="1.9761898512685914in" height="1.9761898512685914in"}

Welcome to the Arahanist Order of Canada.

Whether you have come to this Dhamma hall to participate in chanting, observe the precepts, learn meditation, attend a religious ceremony, or explore the path of renunciation, this book offers an introduction to our religious life and practice.

*Vandanā Pūjā: Homage and Veneration* is both a practical book for chanting and a guide to understanding the spiritual purpose of the Arahanist Order. Its prayers, recollections, precepts, ordination guidelines, and institutional teachings express a common aspiration: to preserve and practise the liberating path taught by Gautama, the Supreme Arahant.

We invite you to read these pages not merely as a collection of religious texts, but as an introduction to a living tradition of ethical discipline, sense restraint, meditation, renunciation, and the pursuit of liberation from worldly bondage.

**Who We Are**

The Arahanist Order of Canada is a renunciant religious community established to preserve and practise the liberation path grounded in the Theravāda Pāli Canon. Our central aspiration is Arahantship: the complete liberation associated with the ending of *rāga*, *dosa*, and *moha*.

We understand the religious life as a sustained process of spiritual transformation. Ethical conduct provides the foundation; sense restraint protects the mind from continual entanglement with worldly concerns; samatha develops concentration and stability; and vipassanā cultivates penetrating insight into worldly experience. Through the integration of *Sīla*, *Samādhi*, and *Paññā*, and renunciation, practitioners undertake the training that leads toward liberation from worldly bondage.

Arahanism takes the renunciant vocation seriously. The full monastic life is not simply a religious title or a change of clothing. It is a commitment to reorganise one\'s life around the Dhamma, relinquish worldly acquisition and attachment, undertake the discipline of the Saṅgha, and devote oneself to spiritual cultivation.

Our name expresses this orientation. We seek to place the liberation taught by Gautama, the Supreme Arahant, at the centre of religious life and to establish a community in which that path can be studied, practised, and transmitted.

**Our Canonical Foundation**

The Arahanist Order draws its primary doctrinal and disciplinary foundation from the Theravāda Pāli Canon, including its teachings on *Sīla*, *Samādhi*, and *Paññā*, renunciation, and monastic discipline.

We approach these teachings as a foundation for practice. Chanting, recollection, and ceremonial homage have their proper place in religious life, but their deeper purpose is fulfilled when they support ethical integrity, clarify the mind, strengthen commitment, and direct the practitioner toward the Dhamma.

For this reason, *Vandanā Pūjā* brings together traditional devotional practices and practical guidance for training. The Three Refuges establish the direction of one\'s commitment. Exalted recollection of the Buddha, Dhamma, and Saṅgha renews confidence in the path. The precepts provide a framework for conduct. Aspirations and the sharing of merits encourage practitioners to dedicate wholesome actions to a purpose greater than immediate personal satisfaction.

These practices are not isolated observances. They belong within a larger discipline of learning, reflection, meditation, and spiritual development.

**A Path of Training for Different Stages of Commitment**

People approach the Dhamma with different circumstances, capacities, and degrees of commitment. The Arahanist Order therefore presents a progression of practice through which individuals can deepen their training.

The Five Precepts establish a foundation of ethical responsibility in daily life. The Uposatha Eight Precepts provide an opportunity to undertake more intensive sense restraint, simplify one\'s activities, and devote greater attention to spiritual cultivation. For those prepared to make a more sustained commitment, Upāsaka and Upāsikā ordination provides a formal setting for dedicated lay training within the religious community.

The novice path of Sāmaṇera and Sāmaṇerī ordination introduces a more comprehensive renunciant discipline. Full Bhikkhu and Bhikkhunī ordination represents a commitment to the training and responsibilities of fully ordained monastic life, grounded in the Vinaya and the Pāṭimokkha.

These forms of commitment have different responsibilities and requirements. They should not be treated as interchangeable. Each calls for sincerity, preparation, and a willingness to undertake the discipline appropriate to it.

The purpose of training is not merely to acquire religious knowledge or institutional status. It is to transform conduct, strengthen concentration, develop wisdom, and progressively abandon the worldly attachments that obstruct liberation.

**Renunciation, Meditation, and Arahantship**

The Arahanist Order places renunciation at the centre of its spiritual orientation. Renunciation means more than withdrawing temporarily from ordinary activities. It involves a deliberate reorientation of one\'s life away from acquisition, sensual entanglement, and the pursuit of worldly satisfaction as the principal aims of existence.

Within this training, sīla, samādhi, and paññā---ethical discipline, concentration, and liberating wisdom---are cultivated together.

Samatha practice develops the stability and concentration of mind needed for sustained contemplation and a higher seeing of the reality. Vipassanā examines experience with discernment, enabling the practitioner to understand multidimensional human existence and shift one's mind away from its conditioned worldly bondage and to weaken the attachments. The two are cultivated within a life of discipline, sense restraint, and renunciant commitment.

Our highest spiritual aspiration is Arahantship, understood as the complete ending of the defilements that sustain bondage to the world. We do not regard the path as merely a method for becoming more comfortable within ordinary life. We seek to preserve the uncompromising religious aspiration toward liberation from worldly bondage itself.

This training requires patience, humility, perseverance, and honest examination. It cannot be reduced to a single ceremony, a particular meditation experience, or an intellectual understanding of the teachings. It calls for the sustained integration of the Dhamma into one\'s conduct, mind, and way of life.

**A Living Religious Community**

The Arahanist Order seeks to establish and sustain communities in which the Dhamma can be practised as a living tradition. A vihara or Dhamma hall serves not only as a place for ceremonies, but also as a setting for chanting, instruction, meditation, ethical training, monastic formation, and mutual support.

Monastics and committed practitioners share responsibility for maintaining conditions conducive to spiritual development. The preservation of the teachings requires study and practice; the preservation of discipline requires accountability; and the continuation of the religious community requires service, material support, and sincere participation.

We also recognise the importance of respectful relations with established Theravāda traditions. Our institutional identity and aspirations do not remove the responsibility to study the canonical inheritance carefully, take monastic discipline seriously, and engage others with honesty and respect.

The Arahanist Order is being developed in Canada with the intention of sustaining a disciplined religious community dedicated to the path of liberation. Its work includes teaching, publication, meditation training, the development of monastic life, and the transmission of the Dhamma to future generations.

**\**

**How to Use This Book**

You may use *Vandanā Pūjā* during communal chanting, personal recollection, the observance of precepts, or the preparation for religious ceremonies. You may also read it as an introduction to the principles and training of the Arahanist Order.

We encourage you to approach its contents with attention and reflection. When reciting the Three Refuges, consider the direction of your commitment. When undertaking the precepts, consider how they can shape your daily conduct. When participating in chanting, cultivate a mind that is collected and respectful. When reading about ordination, consider the responsibilities and discipline associated with each stage of religious life.

The book also introduces the Order\'s understanding of monastic formation, the Vinaya, the Pāṭimokkha, and the full renunciant path for women. These subjects require careful study and sustained guidance; the relevant chapters provide an introduction to their significance within the Arahanist community.

Above all, let the practices contained in this book encourage a movement from words to conduct, from conduct to mental cultivation, and from mental cultivation to wisdom and liberation.

**Our Invitation**

You are welcome to learn about the Arahanist Order, participate in appropriate religious activities, undertake the precepts, study the Dhamma, and explore the stages of training described in these pages.

You need not begin with a complete understanding of the path. Begin with sincerity. Learn carefully. Practise what you undertake. Allow ethical discipline to become firm, the mind to become concentrated, and wisdom to deepen through direct examination and sustained practice.

May this book help you understand the religious life we seek to establish and the path we aspire to preserve. May the Dhamma be studied and practised with integrity. May renunciant training flourish. May those who sincerely undertake the path progress toward the ending of *rāga*, *dosa*, and *moha*.

**Welcome to the Arahanist Order of Canada---the Liberation Path of Gautama, the Supreme Arahant.**

## Tisarana • Three Refuges {#tisarana-three-refuges .headline-3}

**Namo Tassa Bhagavato Arahato Sammāsambuddhasa (X3)** *Homage to the Blessed One, the Supreme Arahant, the Rightly and Fully Awakened One!*

**Buddhaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Dhammaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Saṁghaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

*I go to the Buddha, Dhamma and Sangha as my refuge.*

**Dutiyampi Buddhaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Dutiyampi Dhammaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Dutiyampi Saṁghaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

*For the second time, I go to the Buddha, Dhamma and Sangha as my refuge.*

**Tatiyampi Buddhaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Tatiyampi Dhammaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Tatiyampi Saṁghaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

*For the third time, I go to the Buddha, Dhamma and Sangha as my refuge.*

## Ratanattay-Anussati • Exalted Recollection of The Triple Gem {#ratanattay-anussati-exalted-recollection-of-the-triple-gem .headline-3}

### Buddha-Anusati • *The Exalted Recollection of the Buddha*

**Iti\'pi so Bhagavā, Arahaṃ, Sammā-Sam-Buddho,**

**Vijjā-Caraṇa-Sampanno, Sugato, Lokavidū,**

**Anuttaro** **Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi^1^,**

**Satthā Deva-Manussānaṃ, Buddho, Bhagavā\'ti.**

*Thus indeed (iti\'pi) he (so) is the Divinely Blessed One (Bhagavā), the Pure One (Arahaṃ), The Rightly and Fully Awakened One (Sammā-Sam-Buddho), Perfected (Sampanno) in Liberating Insight and Virtuous Conduct (Vijjā-Caraṇa), Directed To* *Wholesomeness and Virtuousness (Sugato), Knower of Worlds (Lokavidū), Unsurpassed (Anuttaro) Tamer of Human Dhamma(Charioteer of Human Mental Phenomena)* \[**1**: For a discussion of the two traditional readings, *Purisa-Damma-Sārathi* and *Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi*, and the rationale for adopting the latter in Arahanism, see the Commentary on *Anuttaro Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi*, p 9\]*, Teacher (Satthā) of Devas and Manussā, The Awakened One (Buddho), the Divinely Blessed One (Bhagavā).*

### Dhamma-Anusati • *The Exalted Recollection of the Dhamma*

**Svākkhāto Bhagavatā Dhammo, Sandiṭṭhiko, Akāliko, Ehipassiko, Opanayiko, Paccattaṃ, Veditabbo Viññūhī\'ti.**

*Well-explained (Svākkhāto) is the Dhamma by the Divinely Blessed One, Completely Seen (Sandiṭṭhiko), Timelsss (Akāliko), Come and See! (for yourself!) (Ehipassiko), Leading inward (Opanayiko), To the Attā (Soul-Spirit) (Paccattaṃ), To be Directly Realized (Veditabbo), For One Who Possesses Discernment (Viññūhi).*

### Sangha-Anussati • *The Exalted Recollection of the* *Sangha*

**Suppaṭipanno Bhagavato Sāvakasaṅgho,**

**Ujuppaṭipanno Bhagavato Sāvakasaṅgho,**

**Ñāyappaṭipanno Bhagavato Sāvakasaṅgho,**

**Sāmīcippaṭipanno Bhagavato Sāvakasaṅgho.**

**Yadidaṃ Cattāri Purisa-Yugāni Aṭṭha Purisapuggalā,**

**Esa Bhagavato Sāvakasaṅgho, Āhuneyyo, Pāhuneyyo, Dakkhiṇeyyo, Añjalikaraṇīyo, Anuttaraṃ Puññakkhettaṃ Lokassā\'ti.**

*The community of the Bhagavā's disciples (sāvakasaṅgho) are those walking the path of **Wholesomeness and Virtuousness (Sīla)** (suppaṭipanno), The community of the Bhagavā's disciples (sāvakasaṅgho) are those walking the path of **uprightness (Mental directness & integrity)** (ujuppaṭipanno), The community of the Bhagavā's disciples (sāvakasaṅgho) are those walking the path of **direct Liberating Knowledge** (**Ñāṇa**/Paññā)(ñāyappaṭipanno), The community of the Bhagavā's disciples (sāvakasaṅgho) are those walking the path in **total** **alignment and harmony with the Dhamma** (practice and truth become unified) (sāmīcippaṭipanno).*

*Namely (yadidaṃ), the four (cattāri) pairs of persons (purisa-yugāni), the eight (aṭṭha) individual persons (purisapuggalā)---the one who has entered (āpanna) the stream (sota) (Sotāpanna) and the path of stream-entry (Sotāpatti-magga), the one who returns (āgāmī) once (sakid) (Sakadāgāmī) and the path of once-returning (Sakadāgāmī-magga), the non-returner(Anāgāmī) and the path of non-returning(Anāgāmi-magga), and the Arahant, and the path unto Arahantship (Arahatta-magga).*

*This (esa) community of the Bhagavā's disciples (sāvakasaṅgho) is worthy of dedicated offerings/ sacrifices (āhuneyyo), worthy of hospitality (pāhuneyyo), worthy of sacrificial donations (dakkhiṇeyyo), worthy of reverent salutation (añjalikaraṇīyo), an unsurpassed (anuttaraṃ) field of merit (puññakkhettaṃ) for the world (lokassa).*

#### Commentary on Anuttaro Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi: The Unsurpassed Tamer of Human Dhamma

**1. The Misrendering of Anuttaro Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi by Mainstream Pāḷi Scholarship**

The correct expression *Anuttaro Purisa-**Dhamma**-Sārathi*, appearing in the traditional enumeration of the qualities of Gautama, the Supreme Arahant, has been subjected to an erroneous textual and interpretive rendering by mainstream Pāḷi scholarship.

Modern academic publications have predominantly standardized the expression as *Anuttaro Purisa-**Damma**-Sārathi*, interpreting it as referring to the Buddha as the unsurpassed trainer or tamer of human beings. This rendering has been widely propagated through Pāḷi dictionaries, translations, and subsequent academic literature.

A particularly influential example is the *Pali-English Dictionary* compiled by T. W. Rhys Davids and William Stede and published by the Pali Text Society (PTS) between 1921 and 1925. Under the entry ***damma***, the editors explain the expression *purisa-damma-sārathi* as an epithet of the Buddha. Furthermore, they classify the occurrence of *purisa-**dhamma**-sārathi* at **Dīgha Nikāya I.62** as a misprint for *purisa-damma-sārathi*.

This editorial judgement has contributed substantially to the wrong establishment of *damma* as the academically accepted rendering.

**Arahanism rejects this standardization and its resulting interpretation as a serious academic misjudgement, a distortion of the true Dhamma.**

The classification of *dhamma* as a misprint was not a neutral textual correction. It was a judgement made through a particular understanding of the expression, one that failed to recognize the doctrinal significance of *dhamma* in relation to human consciousness and the Buddha\'s own attainment. From the Arahanist standpoint, this reflects an inadequate understanding of the Buddha\'s Dhamma: an established academic mis-interpretation was allowed to determine the reading of the text, while the deeper significance of the rejected reading was overlooked.

The authority of an academic dictionary or editorial convention does not make its conclusions infallible. When an editorial judgement misrepresents the meaning of the Buddha\'s teaching, it must be challenged and corrected. Mainstream academic acceptance cannot substitute for a proper understanding of Dhamma.

More importantly, the difference between *damma* and *dhamma* changes the doctrinal significance of the expression. The former directs the interpretation towards the Buddha\'s ability to train other human beings; the latter concerns the mastery of human dhamma, the mental phenomena arising within individual *citta*.

Arahanism identifies the conventional rendering as a misrepresentation of the true significance of this epithet and restores *Anuttaro Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi* as the proper expression in its Buddhānusati tradition.

The issue is therefore not simply a matter of spelling or translation preference. It concerns the accuracy of the Pāḷi text, the limitations of academic editorial authority, and the preservation of the Buddha\'s authentic liberation teaching.

**2. The Historical Problem: How Was Dhamma Replaced by Damma?**

The existence of *Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi* and *Purisa-Damma-Sārathi* in the history of Pāḷi publication raises an important question concerning textual transmission.

The PTS decision to classify *dhamma* as a misprint effectively established *damma* as the preferred reading. Yet this editorial judgement does not establish the historical origin of the variation. Nor does it prove that the rejected reading is necessarily erroneous. The judgement itself must be examined, particularly in light of the doctrinal implications of the two forms.

The history of Pāḷi literature involves transmission through different regional scripts, manuscript traditions, copying practices, editorial decisions, and systems of transliteration. Errors and alterations can enter this process at different stages.

The emergence of the standardized *damma* reading therefore requires investigation from several perspectives:

- An editorial error may have introduced or reinforced an incorrect reading.

- A copying or transmission error may have altered the original form.

- A historical misunderstanding may have encouraged subsequent editors to prefer a reading consistent with an established interpretation.

- A deliberate alteration, if supported by historical evidence, may have contributed to the displacement of the original expression.

The historical cause must be established through documentary and textual examination. Nevertheless, the consequences of the standardized interpretation are clear: the significance of *dhamma* within this expression has been displaced by an interpretation centred on training other human beings.

Arahanism considers this a matter requiring correction, not merely an academic variation to be accepted without further examination. The investigation must extend beyond the authority of published dictionaries and examine the textual evidence, transmission history, and doctrinal meaning of the expression.

**3. Dhamma: The Mental Phenomena Arising Within Human Citta**

The proper understanding of *Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi* begins with the meaning of *dhamma*.

In the Arahanist understanding, *dhamma* encompasses mental phenomena, qualities, conditions, thoughts, intentions, perceptions, and other manifestations arising within an individual\'s consciousness (*citta*).

The fundamental relationship between mind and mental phenomena is expressed in the opening verse of the *Dhammapada*:

*Manopubbaṅgamā dhammā, manoseṭṭhā manomayā.*

Mind precedes mental phenomena (*dhammā*); mind is their chief, and they are mind-made. This teaching establishes the central importance of understanding the mental phenomena associated with the activity of the mind.

An individual\'s intentions, perceptions, thoughts, ideas, and conduct are conditioned by the *dhammā* arising within *citta*. Unwholesome mental conditions sustain attachment, aversion, delusion, and continued engagement with worldly existence.

Consequently, the fundamental task of spiritual cultivation is the understanding, mastery, and purification of the mind together with how mental phenomena are manifested.

The Buddha\'s attainment demonstrates the complete perfection of this process.

He fully understood the nature of the mental phenomena arising within his consciousness, eradicated *rāga*, *dosa*, and *moha*, and brought the conditions responsible for continued worldly existence to the end.

This is the significance of *Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi*: the unsurpassed mastery of human dhamma, exemplified through the complete purification of the Buddha\'s own *citta*.

**4. The Doctrinal Consequence of the Wrong Rendering**

The difference between the two readings has consequences extending beyond translation.

Under the conventional rendering, *Purisa-Damma-Sārathi* is understood primarily as a description of the Buddha\'s capacity to train, discipline, and guide other individuals.

This places the emphasis on the relationship between the Buddha and those receiving his instruction.

However, the fundamental achievement of the Buddha was his own liberation from worldly bondage. His supreme accomplishment was the complete eradication of the defilements within his own consciousness.

The distinction is particularly important because liberation from worldly bondage is an individual accomplishment. Another person may explain the path, provide instruction, and demonstrate its principles, but cannot purify another individual\'s consciousness or eradicate another person\'s defilements.

The Buddha\'s own attainment is the ultimate demonstration of this truth.

When the expression is rendered *Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi*, its significance is directed towards the mastery and purification of human dhamma. It identifies the Buddha as the unsurpassed example of an individual who has completely mastered the mental phenomena arising within his own consciousness.

The mainstream academic rendering therefore diverts attention from this essential dimension of Gautama Buddha\'s attainment.

The issue is not whether the Buddha taught and guided other human beings. He unquestionably did. The issue is whether this particular epithet should be interpreted as referring primarily to that activity or to the complete mastery of human dhamma demonstrated through his own liberation.

Arahanism affirms the latter as the true understanding.

**5. Restoration of the True Dhamma: The Arahanist Responsibility**

One of the fundamental missions of Arahanism is the restoration and preservation of the true Dhamma of Gautama, the Supreme Arahant.

This responsibility extends beyond the preservation of inherited religious practices. It requires critical examination of the Pāḷi Canon, its textual transmission, terminology, translations, and subsequent interpretations.

Established academic conventions must not be treated as unquestionable authorities when their conclusions conflict with the proper understanding of the Buddha\'s teaching. Editorial decisions must be examined, textual errors must be corrected, and misunderstandings must be brought to light.

The Buddha himself identified his noble bhikkhu disciples as *bhagavato putto oraso mukhato jāto dhammajo dhammanimmito dhammadāyādo*---sons of the Blessed One, born from his mouth, born of the Dhamma, created by the Dhamma, and heirs to the Dhamma. This declaration appears in suttas such as the *Aggañña Sutta* (DN 27).

As a Theravāda bhikkhu and a son and heir of Gautama Buddha\'s Dhamma, Bhante Mudita undertakes the responsibility of preserving, examining, and correcting the understanding of Gautama Buddha\'s teaching. This responsibility cannot be fulfilled by simply accepting established academic conclusions when they obscure the doctrinal significance of the original teaching.

The history of religious literature demonstrates that the transmission of teachings is not immune to human error, interpretive bias, or historical alteration. Whether a particular departure arose accidentally or deliberately must be determined through evidence. Its consequences, however, must not be allowed to obscure the original teaching.

The restoration of *Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi* forms part of this responsibility.

Arahanism seeks to preserve the fundamental direction of Gautama\'s teaching: individual purification through the eradication of *rāga*, *dosa*, and *moha*, culminating in liberation from worldly bondage.

This is not simply a question of correcting one Pāḷi expression. It concerns the preservation of the doctrinal understanding conveyed by the language of the Pāḷi Canon and the restoration of Gautama Buddha\'s authentic liberation teaching.

**6. The True Rendering: Anuttaro Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi**

Arahanism therefore retains the expression:

***Anuttaro Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi.***

**The Unsurpassed Tamer of Human Dhamma.**

The expression venerates Gautama as the unsurpassed master of human dhamma, exemplifying the complete mastery and purification of his own consciousness.

His attainment demonstrates the ultimate possibility of individual spiritual perfection: the eradication of *rāga*, *dosa*, and *moha*, the cessation of worldly entanglement, and the realization of Nibbāna, the deathless realm.

The proper understanding of this epithet must therefore remain connected to the Buddha\'s own attainment and the fundamental objective of his liberation teaching.

The restoration of *Purisa-Dhamma-Sārathi* is one contribution of Arahanism to the preservation, examination, and restoration of the true Dhamma.

## Paritta Paritāṇa • Protective Shielding

*The Sacred Recitation of Protection, Blessings, and Victory*

**Paritta-Paritāṇa** signifies the protective power of sacred Dhamma recitation: the invocation of truth, the recollection of noble qualities, and the establishment of a sanctuary of virtue, loving-kindness, and spiritual confidence. In the Arahanist tradition, protective chanting is not merely the vocal repetition of ancient verses. It is a devotional and contemplative practice through which the practitioner brings body, speech, and mind into harmony with the liberating Dhamma of Gautama, the Supreme Arahant.

The Pāḷi word *paritta* denotes protection or a safeguard, with Pari- (encircling, all-around) + Tāṇa (shield, refuge, shelter, defense). Together, these terms express the aspiration to establish a protective refuge through the power of the Dhamma. Such protection is cultivated through confidence in truth, the recollection of noble virtue, the development of *mettā* (loving-kindness), and the purification and concentration of *citta*, the mind-heart.

The efficacy of protective recitation is inseparable from the quality of the practitioner. Words chanted without understanding may remain merely sounds; when recited with understanding, devotion, composure, and a mind established in wholesome qualities, they become an occasion for profound recollection and transformation. *Sīla* (virtue) provides the foundation, *samādhi* (concentration) elevates the mind, and *paññā* (wisdom) illuminates the nature of experience. Through these qualities, protective chanting becomes more than a ceremony: it becomes a practice of strengthening the mind against fear, confusion, hostility, and the unwholesome influences that disturb inner peace.

In traditional Theravāda communities, *paritta* recitation has long accompanied important occasions in human life, including ordinations, the dedication of religious buildings, illness, times of uncertainty, and communal gatherings. The chanting of sacred discourses expresses confidence in the Dhamma and invokes blessings for safety, harmony, prosperity in wholesome qualities, and freedom from adversity. In some traditions, protective recitations are also accompanied by candles, consecrated water, sacred thread, and other ritual observances. These practices give visible and communal expression to the aspiration that the Dhamma may surround and sustain the lives of those who place their confidence in it.

The protective suttas presented in this chapter embody complementary dimensions of this sacred practice. The *Mahā Maṅgala Sutta* sets forth the highest blessings through ethical conduct, learning, service, restraint, and spiritual development. The *Karaṇīya Mettā Sutta* teaches the immeasurable cultivation of loving-kindness toward all beings. The *Ratana Sutta* proclaims the excellence of the Triple Gem and the qualities that make the Dhamma a supreme refuge. The *Mahā Jayamaṅgala Gāthā* and *Jaya Maṅgala Gāthā* celebrate Gautama Buddha\'s victories through wisdom, patience, compassion, and spiritual mastery.

Together, these recitations establish a movement from blessing to purification, from loving-kindness to confidence, and from confidence to the cultivation of noble strength. Their purpose is not simply to secure favourable circumstances within the world, but also to encourage the qualities by which the practitioner becomes increasingly independent of fear, craving, aversion, and delusion.

For the Arahanist, the deepest protection is found in the gradual liberation of the mind from the causes of suffering. External safeguards may offer reassurance, and sacred recitation may strengthen confidence and composure, but the supreme refuge is realised through the actual practice of the Dhamma. A mind established in virtue is less vulnerable to remorse; a mind suffused with loving-kindness is less governed by hostility; a mind developed in concentration is less scattered by disturbance; and a mind illuminated by wisdom increasingly understands the path to liberation from worldly bondage.

Thus, **Paritta-Paritāṇa is both a sacred invocation of protection and a reminder of the path toward the highest spiritual refuge**. Through reverent recitation, the practitioner honours the truth, recollects the qualities of the noble ones, radiates goodwill toward living beings, and strengthens the determination to walk the path of renunciation and liberation.

May these sacred recitations bring peace to the mind, harmony among living beings, protection from unwholesome conditions, and steadfast confidence in the liberating Dhamma.

### Ratana Suttaṁ • The Sutta on That Which Brings Supreme Delight, Absolute Value, and Transcendent Beauty 

**Ratana** (*n.*): Derived from the root *Ram* ("to take supreme delight in"). It denotes that which evokes supreme spiritual delight, unblemished beauty, and ultimate value---referring intrinsically to the transcendent qualities of Gautama Buddha, his Dhamma, and his Bhikkhu and Bhikkhunī Saṅgha.

**1. Yānīdha bhūtāni samāgatāni, Bhūmmāni vā yāni va antalikkhe; Sabb\'eva bhūtā sumanā bhavantu, Atho pi sakkacca sunantu bhāsitaṁ.**

*Whatever living beings, spirits or elemental entities (bhūtāni) are gathered here---whether terrestrial (Bhūmi) or in the Intermediary Realm (sky, to the refined, higher-dimensional celestial spheres- Sagga/ Devaloka)---may all beings (bhūtā) without exception become gladdened and well-disposed in heart, and moreover, let them listen with earnest reverence to this sacred utterance of truth.*

**2. Tasmā hi bhūtā nisāmetha sabbe, Mettaṁ karotha mānusiyā pajāya; Divā ca ratto ca haranti ye baliṁ, Tasmā hi ne rakkhatha appamattā.**

*Therefore, O all beings, listen closely, all of you! Extend loving-kindness to the progeny of Mānusa (human race); for day and night they bring you devotional offerings (bali)---therefore, protect (rakkhatha) them with diligent vigilance (appamatta).*

**3. Yaṁ kiñci vittaṁ idha vā huraṁ vā, Saggesu vā yaṁ ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ; Na no samaṁ atthi Tathāgatena, Idam pi Buddhe ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ; Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*Whatever wealth (vitta) here on earth (Idha) or in* *future existence (Hura), or whatever exalted precious gem (ratana) in the celestial heavens (Sagga/* *Svarga: Devaloka/ Brahmaloka)---there is none equal (Sama) to the Tathāgata. Regarding the Buddha, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being (Suvatthi)!*

**4. Khayaṁ virāgaṁ amataṁ paṇītaṁ, Yad ajjhagā Sakyamunī samāhito; Na tena Dhammena sam\'atthi kiñci, Idam pi Dhamme ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ; Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*The exhaustion \[of craving and taints\], dispassion, the sublime deathless---which the Sakyamuni attained in Samādhi---there is nothing equal (sama) to that Dhamma. Regarding the Dhamma, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being (Suvatthi)!*

**5. Yaṁ Buddhaseṭṭho parivaṇṇayī suciṁ, Samādim ānantarikañ-ñamāhu; Samādhinā tena samo na vijjati, Idam pi Dhamme ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ; Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*That purity which the most excellent Buddha praised, and which they declared to be the uninterrupted Samādhi---there is nothing equal (Samo) to that Samādhi. Regarding the Dhamma, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being (Suvatthi)!*

**6. Ye puggalā aṭṭha sataṁ pasatthā, Cattāri etāni yugāni honti; Te dakkhiṇeyyā Sugatassa sāvakā, Etesu dinnāni mahapphalāni; Idam pi Saṅghe ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ, Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*Those eight persons praised by the wise---these are four successive pairs. They are the disciples of the Sugato (Directed to Wholesomeness and Virtuousness), worthy of offerings (dakkhiṇeyyā), that offered to them yield great fruit. Regarding the Saṅgha, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being (Suvatthi)!*

**7. Ye suppayuttā manasā daḷhena, Nikkāmino Gotama sāsanamhi; Te pattipattā amataṁ vigayha, Laddhā mudhā nibbutiṁ bhuñjamānā; Idam pi Saṅghe ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ, Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*Those who are firm with the mind (manasā) thoroughly yoked in practice (suppayuttā), free from kāma (nikkāmino) in the teaching of Gautama---they have attained (pattipattā), and immerse (vigayha) in the Deathless, having obtained that (laddhā), they enjoy (Bhuñjamānā) graciously (mudhā) the cooling of fires \[rāga, dosa, and moha\] (Nibbuti). Regarding the Saṅgha, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being (Suvatthi)!*

**8. Yathindakhīlo paṭhaviṁ sito siyā, Catubbhi vātebhi asampakampiyo; Tathūpamaṁ sappurisaṁ vadāmi, Yo ariyasaccāni avecca passati; Idam pi Saṅghe ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ, Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*Just as (Yathā) Indra's pillar (Indakhīlo), firmly sunk (sito) into the earth (paṭhavī), Would be (siyā) immovable by winds from four directions (catubbhi)---* *similar to that (tathā-upamā), such a noble/true person (sappurisa) I declare him to be, who sees (passati) and directly penetrates / comprehends with unwavering certainty (avecca) the Noble Truths (Ariya-Saccā). Regarding the Saṅgha, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being (Suvatthi)!*

**9. Ye ariya-saccāni vibhāvayanti, Gambhīra-paññena sudesitāni; Kiñcāpi te honti bhusāppamattā, Na te bhavaṁ aṭṭhamaṁ ādiyanti; Idam pi Saṅghe ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ, Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*Those who thoroughly comprehend (vibhāvayanti) the Noble Truths (Ariya-Sacca),* *well-taught (sudesitāni) by the One of deep wisdom (gambhīra-paññā)---moreover even, as they are intensely and abundantly vigilant (bhusa-appamattā), never do they take an eighth existence in saṃsāra. Regarding the Saṅgha, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being!*

**10. Sahāva'ssa dassana sampadāya, Tayassu dhammā jahitā bhavanti; Sakkāya-diṭṭhi vicikicchitañ ca, Sīlabbataṁ vā'pi yadatthi kiñci; Catūhapāyehi ca vippamutto, Cha cābhiṭhānāni abhabbo kātuṁ; Idam pi Saṅghe ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ; Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*At the very moment of his perfection in seeing (dassana) \[the Noble Truths\], indeed three states (Dhammā) are abandoned: true-body view (Sakkāya-diṭṭhi) (**note**:* *whereas erroneous contemporary Buddhist interpretations wrongly translate this as "Self-identity view---the belief that a soul resides within the aggregates," Arahanism recognizes that humans **are** soul-spirits experiencing a physical manifestation), skeptical doubt (vicikicchita) \[the deathless reality and path of liberation\], and blind attachment to the observance (bata) of Sīla (Sīlabbata) \[merit hunter or overly fear for observance-failures, abused by the Jataka stories beyond moral teaching, not knowing Sīla is training ground for gradual withdrawal from the world.\] He is completely liberated from the four woeful states, and is incapable of committing the six heinous crimes. Regarding the Saṅgha, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being (Suvatthi)!*

**11. Kiñcā'pi so kammaṁ karoti pāpakaṁ, Kāyena vācā uda cetasā vā; Abhabbo so tassa paṭicchādāya, Abhabbatā diṭṭha-padassa vuttā; Idam pi Saṅghe ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ, Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*Even though he might perform some unwholesome action, by way of body, speech, or the mind, he is incapable of concealing it. Such incapacity (abhabbatā) has been declared (vuttā) for one who has seen (diṭṭha) the step / path (pada) \[of Noble Truth\]. Regarding the Saṅgha, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being (Suvatthi)!*

**12. Vanappagumbe yathā phussitagge, Gimhānamāse paṭhamasmiṁ gimhe; Tathūpamaṁ Dhamma-varaṁ adesayi, Nibbāṇagāmiṁ paramaṁ hitāya; Idam pi Buddhe ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ; Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*Just as a dense forest bush with its blossoming crown bursts into flower, during the first month of summer---even so comparable is the supreme Dhamma which He taught, leading directly to Nibbāna for the highest welfare of all. Regarding the Buddha, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being (Suvatthi)!*

**13. Varo varaññū varado varāharo, Anuttaro Dhamma-varaṁ adesayi; Idam pi Buddhe ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ, Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*The Best One (varo), the Knower of the Best (varaññū), the Giver of the Best (varado), the Bringer of the Best (varāharo)---the unsurpassed one (anuttaro) taught the best Dhamma. Regarding the Buddha, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being (Suvatthi)!*

**14. Khīṇaṁ purāṇaṁ navaṁ natthi sambhavaṁ, viratta-cittā āyatike bhavasmiṁ; Te khīṇa-bījā avirūḷhi-chandā, Nibbanti dhīrā yathāyaṁ padīpo; Idam pi Saṅghe ratanaṁ paṇītaṁ, Etena saccena suvatthi hotu.**

*The old \[karma / past actions\] is exhausted (khīṇa), no new \[karma\] coming to arise (Sambhava); with mind-heart **cittā** completely detached toward future existence, their seeds \[of future rebirth\] (bīja) exhausted (khīṇa), they have no desires (chanda) for growth \[in saṃsāra\] (virūḷhi), the wise (dhīrā) cool down \[the fire of rāga, dosa, and moha\] (Nibbanti), just as (yathā) this (ayaṁ) lamp (padīpo). Regarding the Saṅgha, indeed this is a supreme Jewel; by the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being (Suvatthi)!*

**15. Yānīdha bhūtāni samāgatāni, Bhummāni vā yāniva antaḷikkhe; Tathāgataṁ devamanussa-pūjitaṁ, Buddhaṁ namassāma suvatthi hotu.**

*Whatever beings are assembled here---whether terrestrial or inhabiting the sky atmospheric interdimensional continuum---let us pay homage to the **Buddha**, the Tathāgata, revered/ worshiped (Pūjita) by both devas and Manussa. May there be safety and well-being!*

**16. Yānīdha bhūtāni samāgatāni, Bhummāni vā yāniva antaḷikkhe; Tathāgataṁ devamanussa-pūjitaṁ, Dhammaṁ namassāma suvatthi hotu.**

*Whatever beings are assembled here---whether terrestrial or inhabiting the sky atmospheric interdimensional continuum---let us pay homage to the **Dhamma** revealed by the Tathāgata, revered/ worshiped (Pūjita) by both devas and Manussa. May there be safety and well-being!*

**17. Yānīdha bhūtāni samāgatāni, Bhummāni vā yāniva antaḷikkhe; Tathāgataṁ devamanussa-pūjitaṁ, Saṅghaṁ namassāma suvatthi hotu.**

*Whatever beings are assembled here---whether terrestrial or inhabiting the sky atmospheric interdimensional continuum---let us pay homage to the **Saṅgha,** established by the Tathāgata, revered/ worshiped (Pūjita) by both devas and Manussa. May there be safety and well-being!*

**Etena saccena suvatthi hotu!**

*By the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being!\*

### Maha Maṅgala Suttaṁ • The Sutta on the Great Blessings 

**Evaṁ me sutaṁ: Ekaṁ samayaṁ Bhagavā, Sāvatthiyaṁ viharati Jetavane, Anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Atha kho aññatarā devata, abhikkantāya rattiyā, abhikkan­tavaṇṇā kevalakappaṁ, Jetavanaṁ obhāsetvā, yena Bhagavā tenupasaṅkami. Upas­aṅkamitvā Bhagavantaṁ abhivādetvā, ekamantaṁ aṭṭhāsi. Ekamantaṁ ṭhitā kho sā devata, Bhagavantaṁ gāthāya ajjhabhāsi:**

*Thus by me it was heard. On one occasion the Blessed One is dwelling in Sāvatthī, in Jeta\'s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's monastery. Then a certain devatā of surpassing beauty, late in the night, having illuminated the entire Jeta\'s Grove, approached where the Blessed One was. Having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, she stood to one side. Standing to one side, that devatā addressed the Blessed One in verse:*

Commentary: Anāthapiṇḍika was a wealthy merchant and chief patron of Gautama Buddha in Sāvatthī. His given name was **Sudatta**. He was given the honorary title *Anāthapiṇḍika* (*\"Giver of alms to the helpless\"* or *\"Feeder of the destitute\"*) by the local people because of his extraordinary generosity in feeding the poor, orphans, and destitute. He was an extremely prosperous householder (*gahapati*) and merchant prince (*setthi*) residing in Sāvatthī, the capital of the Kosala Kingdom. He purchased the land known as **Jetavana** (*Jeta\'s Grove*) from **Prince Jeta** (who *was* royal, being a prince of Kosala) by covering the ground with gold coins (*kahāpaṇas*), and built the monastery where the Buddha spent 19 rainy seasons.

**\**

**1. Bahū devā manussā ca, Maṅgalāni acintayuṁ;**

**Ākaṅkhamānā sotthānaṁ, Brūhi maṅgalam-uttamaṁ.**

*Many devas and manussas have pondered on blessings (maṅgala), desiring well-being (sotthānaṁ). Speak the supreme blessing!*

**2. Asevanā ca bālānaṁ, Paṅḍitānañ ca sevanā;**

**Pūjā ca pūjanīyānaṁ, Etaṁ maṅgalam-uttamaṁ.**

*Non-association with fools, and association with the wise; and veneration of those worthy of veneration: this is the supreme blessing.*

**3. Patirūpa-desa-vāso ca, Pubbe ca kata-puññatā;**

**Atta-sammā-paṇīdhi ca, Etaṁ maṅgalam-uttamaṁ.**

*Dwelling in a suitable locality, having done wholesome deeds in the past; and perfectly aligning your Atta (soul-spirit): this is the supreme blessing.*

Commentary: Here I want to intentionally preserve the transcendental, metaphysical *Atta* (**\"soul-spirit\"** or **\"inner spirit\"**) and strictly avoid modern psychological reductionism of translating *atta* as \"self\", because English words like \"self\" carry too much psychological baggage.

Historically, early Western translators of Pali (like T.W. Rhys Davids) frequently wrestled with this exact linguistic tension. By translating *atta* to **\"soul-spirit\"**, I purposely shift the meaning of the verse from modern self-help psychology back into the realm of **classical spiritual cultivation**.

Here is how the etymological breakdown of *Atta-sammā-paṇīdhi*:

1.  **Atta:** as the **soul-spirit**, which aligns directly with its Vedic ancestor *ātman*---the vital, innermost core of a living being, the spark of consciousness, or the spiritual locus of an individual.

2.  **Sammā:** Rather than just \"correct behavior,\" *sammā* becomes **\"divinely aligned,\" \"perfectly tuned,\"** or **\"harmonized with Ultimate Truth.\"**

3.  **Paṇīdhi:** Instead of a psychological \"habit switch,\" *paṇīdhi* becomes the **exalted orientation, anchoring, or alignment** of that soul-spirit toward the highest reality.

**Scholarly Comparisons**

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Translator**                **Translation of the Verse**
  ----------------------------- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Our Translation**           *\"Dwelling in a suitable locality, having done wholesome deeds in the past; and* *perfectly aligning your Atta (soul-spirit): this is the supreme blessing.\"*

  **Bhikkhu Bodhi**\            *\"Living in a suitable region, having made merit in the past, and **right self-aspiration**: this is the foremost blessing.\"*
  *(The Suttanipata, Oxford)*   

  **Ācārya Buddharakkhita**\    *\"To reside in a suitable locality, to have done meritorious actions in the past, and to **set oneself in the right course** --- this is the greatest blessing.\"*
  *(Maha Bodhi Society)*        

  **Thanissaro Bhikkhu**\       *\"Living in a civilized land, having made merit in the past, **directing oneself rightly**: this is the highest protection.\"*
  *(Access to Insight)*         
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**\**

**4. Bāhu-saccañca sippañca, Vinayo ca susikkhito;**

**Subhāsitā ca yā vācā, Etaṁ maṅgalam-uttamaṁ.**

*Deep knowledge, and Professional craft skills, and a well-trained discipline; and speech that is well-spoken: this is the supreme blessing.*

**5. Mātāpitū upaṭṭhānaṁ, Putta-dārassa saṅgaho;**

**Anākulā ca kammantā, Etaṁ maṅgalam-uttamaṁ.**

*Supporting parents, holding together (saṅgaho) child and wife; and unentangled work: this is the supreme blessing.*

**6. Dānañca dhamma-cariyā ca, Ñātakānañ ca saṅgaho;**

**Anavajjāni kammāni, Etaṁ maṅgalam-uttamaṁ.**

*Generosity, and living by Dhamma, and holding together relatives; blameless actions: this is the supreme blessing.*

**7. Ārati virati pāpā, Majjapānā ca saññamo;**

**Appamādo ca dhammesu, Etaṁ maṅgalam-uttamaṁ.**

*Abstinence and refraining from evil, and away from drinking intoxicants; and spiritual zeal (appamādo) regarding mental qualities (dhammas): this is the supreme blessing.*

**8. Gāravo ca nivāto ca, Santuṭṭhī ca kataññutā;**

**Kālena dhamma-savanaṁ, Etaṁ maṅgalam-uttamaṁ.**

*Reverence, and humility, and contentment, and gratitude; listening to the Dhamma at the right time: this is the supreme blessing.*

**\**

**9. Khantī ca sovacassatā, Samaṇānañca dassanaṁ;**

**Kālena dhamma-sākacchā, Etaṁ maṅgalam-uttamaṁ.**

*Patience, and gentleness, and visiting samaṇas; and Dhamma discussion at the right time: this is the supreme blessing.*

**10. Tapo ca brahmacariyañca, Ariya-Saccāna-dassanaṁ;**

**Nibbāṇa-sacchi-kiriyā ca, Etaṁ maṅgalam-uttamaṁ.**

*Tapas (primordial spiritual force) and Brahmacariya, seeing the Noble Truths; and realizing/seeing (sacchi) Nibbāna: this is the supreme blessing.*

**11. Phuṭṭhassa loka-dhammehi, Cittaṁ yassa na kampati;**

**Asokaṁ virajaṁ khemaṁ, Etaṁ maṅgalam-uttamaṁ**

*Touched by worldly existence, whose Citta does not waver; sorrowless, stainless, safe: this is the supreme blessing.*

**12. Etādisāni katvāna, Sabbattha maparājitā; Sabbattha sotthiṁ gacchanti taṁ, Tesaṁ maṅgalam-uttaman'ti.**

*Having accomplished such things, won't be defeated in all place; everywhere they go is in safety: that for them is the supreme blessing.*

**Etena saccena suvatthi hotu!**

*By the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being!*

### Karanīya Metta Suttaṁ • The Sutta on What Ought to be Done for Kindness 

**1. Karaṇīyam-attha-kusalena, Yaṁ taṁ santaṁ padaṁ abhisamecca; Sakko ujū ca sūjū ca, Suvaco cassa mudu anatimānī.**

*This is to be done by one who knows what is good, who wishes to completely unify with that supreme peaceful footing/abode \[Nibbāna\]: competent and upright, and thoroughly upright, easy to speak to, and should be gentle, not arrogant.*

**2. Santussako ca subharo ca, Appakicco ca sa-lahuka-vutti; Santindriyo ca nipako ca, Appagabbho kulesu ananugiddho.**

*Contented, and easily supported, and unburdened, living lightly; and peaceful in senses, and prudent, not rude, not greedily attached among favorite households.*

Commentary: Subtle trap of getting too emotionally tangled, cozy, or greedy for favors, food, and praise from specific favorite houses. By remaining free from rudeness (**appagabbho**) yet not greedily attached among favorite households (**kulesu ananugiddho**), the bhikkhu maintains his absolute independence, dignity, and clean spiritual boundaries as he moves through the material world.

**3. Na ca khuddhaṁ samācare kiñci, Yena viññū pare upavadeyyuṁ; Sukhino vā khemino hontu, Sabbe-sattā bhavantu sukhita-attā.**

*And one should not commit any minor transgression by which the wise others might criticize; \[May they think:\] \"May all beings be happy and safe, may they become happy in attā (soul-spirit)!*

**4. Ye keci pāṇabhūta-atthī, Tasā vā thāvarā vā anavasesā; Dīghā vā ye mahantā vā, Majjhimā rassakā-aṇuka-thūlā.**

*Whatever living beings there are---weak or strong, all-inclusive; long (e.g., snakes, eels), or large (e.g., elephants, whales), medium (e.g., dogs, humans), short (e.g., frogs, small birds), tiny* *(e.g., insects, microbes invisible to the physical eye), or gross / fat (e.g., bulky creatures, stout animals).*

**5. Diṭṭhā vā ye va addiṭṭhā, Ye ca dūre vasanti avidūre; Bhūtā vā sambhavesī vā,Sabbe-sattā bhavantu sukhita-attā.**

*Seen or unseen, dwelling far or near; born or* *to be born (egg, womb-* *embryo or fetus, wandering spirits)---may all beings become happy in attā (soul-spirit)!*

**6. Na paro paraṁ nikubbetha, Nātimaññetha katthaci naṁ kiñci; Byārosanā paṭighasaññā, Nāññamaññassa dukkham-iccheyya.**

*Let not one deceive another, nor despise anyone anywhere at all. Through anger or perception of aversion, let not one wish suffering toward another.*

**7. Mātā yathā niyaṁ puttaṁ, Āyusā ekaputtam-anurakkhe; Evam'pi sabba-bhūtesu, Mānasaṁ bhāvaye aparimāṇaṁ.**

*Just as a mother would protect her own child, her only child, with her life; so also one should develop a boundless (aparimāṇa) mind (mānasa) toward all beings.*

**\**

**8. Mettaṁ ca sabba-lokasmiṁ, Mānasaṁ bhāvaye aparimāṇaṁ; Uddhaṁ adho ca tiriyañca, Asaṁbādhaṁ averaṁ asapattaṁ.**

*And kindness toward all beings in the world---one should develop a boundless mind: above and below, and across, unhindered, without hatred (internal, one's own inner space of the toxic fire of malice; harbor no poison of hatred toward anyone), without rivalry (external, one's social friction, being gentle, non-threatening posture dissolves the very conditions that cause others to oppose or attack you; actively cross no lines; have no rivals or enemies).*

**9. Tiṭṭhaṁ caraṁ nisinno vā, Sayāno vā yāvata'ssa vigatamiddho, Etaṁ satiṁ adhiṭṭheyya; Brahmam-etaṁ vihāraṁ idham-āhu.**

*Standing, walking, or seated, or lying down, as long as one is free from drowsiness, one should establish this mindfulness; this, they say, is the abode (vihāra) of Brahma, in this teaching.*

**10. Diṭṭhiṁ ca anupagamma sīlavā, Dassanena sampanno; Kāmesu vineyya gedhaṁ, Na hi jātu gabbha-seyyaṁ punaretī'ti.**

*Not clinging to dogma (diṭṭhi), possessing sīla, perfected in vision; having removed desire for sexual/ sensual pleasures (kāmesu)---indeed, one never returns again to rebirth in a womb.*

**Etena saccena suvatthi hotu!**

*By the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being!*

### Mahā Jaya-maṅgala Gāthā • Stanzas of Great Joyful Victory 

> **Mahākāruṇiko nātho, hitāya sabba-pāṇīnaṁ,**
>
> **Pūretvā pāramī sabbā, patto sambodhim-uttamaṁ;**
>
> **Etena sacca-vajjena, hotu te jaya-maṅgalaṁ.**

*The Great-Compassionate Protector, for the welfare of all living beings, having fulfilled all the pāramīs, attained supreme full awakening. By the utterance of this truth, may joyful victory be yours!*

> **Jayanto bodhiyā mule, sakyānaṁ nandi-vaḍḍhano;**
>
> **Evaṁ tuyhaṁ jayo hotu, jayassu jaya-maṅgalaṁ.**

*Victorious at the foot of the Bodhi tree, he increased the delight of the Sakyans; So let there be victory for you. Be victorious! A joyful victory!*

> **Sakkatvā Buddha-ratanaṁ, osadhaṁ uttamaṁ varaṁ,**
>
> **Hitaṁ deva-manussānaṁ; Buddha-tejena sotthinā,**
>
> **Nassantū-paddavā sabbe, dukkhā vūpasamentu te.**

*Having revered the Buddha-gem---the supreme, preferred medicine, beneficial to devas and manussas---by the majesty-power of the Buddha, in safety, may all afflictions perish, may your dukkhas cease!*

> **Sakkatvā Dhamma-ratanaṁ, osadhaṁ uttamaṁ varaṁ,**
>
> **Pariḷāh-ūpasamaṇaṁ; Dhamma-tejena sotthinā,**
>
> **Nassantū-paddavā sabbe, bhayā vūpasamentu te.**

*Having revered the Dhamma-gem---the supreme, preferred medicine, the appeaser of feverish burning---by the majesty-power of the Dhamma, in safety, may all afflictions perish, may your fears cease!*

> **Sakkatvā Saṅgharatanaṁ, osadhaṁ uttamaṁ varaṁ,**
>
> **Āhuneyyaṁ pāhuneyyaṁ; Saṅghatejena sotthinā,**
>
> **Nassantu paddavā sabbe, rogā vūpasamentu te.**

*Having revered the Saṅgha-gem---the supreme, preferred medicine, worthy of sacrificial offerings, worthy of hospitality---by the majesty-power of the Saṅgha, in safety, may all afflictions perish, may your diseases cease!*

> **Yaṁ kiṇci ratanaṁ loke, vijjati vividhā puthu;**
>
> **Ratanaṁ Buddha-samaṁ natthi. tasmā sotthi bhavantu te.**

*Whatever jewel exists in the world, different of various kinds; there is no jewel equal to the Buddha. Therefore, let there be well-being for you!*

> **Yaṁ kiṇci ratanaṁ loke, vijjati vividhā puthu; Ratanaṁ Dhamma-samaṁ natthi. tasmā sotthi bhavantu te.**

*Whatever jewel exists in the world, different of various kinds; there is no jewel equal to the Dhamma. Therefore, let there be well-being for you!*

> **Yaṁ kiṇci ratanaṁ loke, vijjati vividhā puthu;**
>
> **Ratanaṁ Saṅgha-samaṁ natthi. tasmā sotthi bhavantu te.**

*Whatever jewel exists in the world, different of various kinds; there is no jewel equal to the Saṅgha. Therefore, let there be well-being for you!*

> **Natthi me saraṇaṁ aññaṁ, Buddho me saraṇaṁ varaṁ;**
>
> **Etena sacca-vajjena, hotu te jaya-maṅgalaṁ.**

*For me there is no other refuge, the Buddha is my choice refuge; By this utterance of truth, may joyful victory be yours!*

> **Natthi me saraṇaṁ aññaṁ, Dhammo me saraṇaṁ varaṁ;**
>
> **Etena sacca-vajjena, hotu te jaya-maṅgalaṁ.**

*For me there is no other refuge; the Dhamma is my choice refuge; By this utterance of truth, may joyful victory be yours!*

> **Natthi me saraṇaṁ aññaṁ, Sangho me saraṇaṁ varaṁ;**
>
> **Etena sacca-vajjena, hotu te jaya-maṅgalaṁ.**

*For me there is no other refuge; the Saṅgha is my choice refuge. By this utterance of truth, may joyful victory be yours!*

> **Sabbītiyo vivajjantu, sabbarogo vinassatu;**
>
> **Mā te bhavatv-antarāyo, sukhī dīghāyukho bhava.**

*May all distresses turn away; may all disease vanish. Let there be no danger for you; be happy and long-lived!*

> **Bhavatu sabba-maṅgalaṁ, rakkhantu sabba-devatā;**
>
> **Sabba Buddhānubhāvena, sadā sotthi bhavantu te.**

*May there be all auspiciousness, may all devas protect; by the power of the Buddhas, may there always be well-being for you!*

> **Bhavatu sabba-maṅgalaṁ, rakkhantu sabba-devatā;**
>
> **Sabba Dhammānubhāvena, sadā sotthi bhavantu te.**

*May there be all auspiciousness, may all devas protect; by the power of all the Dhamma, may there always be well-being for you!*

> **Bhavatu sabba-maṅgalaṁ, rakkhantu sabba-devatā;**
>
> **Sabba Saṅghānubhāvena, sadā sotthi bhavantu te.**

*May there be all auspiciousness, may all devas protect; by the power of all the Saṅgha, may there always be well-being for you!*

**Etena saccena suvatthi hotu!**

*By the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being!*

### Jaya Mangala Gāthā • Stanzas of Joyful Victory of the Buddha 

**1. Bāhuṁ sahassam abhinimmitasā'yudhan taṁ, Girimekhalaṁ udita-ghora-sa-sena-māraṁ;**

**Dānādi-Dhamma-vidhinā jitavā Munindo,**

**Taṁ tejasā bhavatu te jaya-maṅgalāni.**

*Māra---who created a thousand arms equipped with weapons, riding the elephant Girimekhala with his fierce army risen up---the Lord of Muni (the Buddha) conquered by means of Dhamma qualities beginning with generosity (dāna). By that power, may joyful victories be yours!*

**2. Mārātirekam abhiyujjhita sabba rattiṁ,**

**Ghoraṁ panālavakam akkha-mathaddha-yakkhaṁ;**

**Khantī sudanta-vidhinā jitavā Munindo,**

**Taṁ tejasā bhavatu te jaya-maṅgalāni.**

*Māra-surpassing, attacked throughout the entire night, the fierce, moreover, Āḷavaka---the stiff-eyed yakkha; the Lord of Muni (the Buddha) conquered him by means of the method of well-tamed (sudanta) patience (khanti). By that power, may joyful victories be yours!*

**3. Nālāgiriṁ gaja-varaṁ atimatta-bhūtaṁ,**

**Dāvāggi cakkam-asanīva sudāruṇan-taṁ;**

**Mettāmbuseka-vidhinā jitavā Munindo,**

**Taṁ tejasā bhavatu te jaya-maṅgalāni.**

*Nāḷāgiri---the excellent elephant that had become excessively frenzied, extremely dreadful like a forest fire, a wheel, or a thunderbolt---the Lord of Muni (the Buddha) conquered him by means of sprinkling the water of kindness (mettā). By that power, may joyful victories be yours!*

**4. Ukkhitta-khaggam-ati-hattha sudāruṇan taṁ,**

**Dhāvanti-yojana-pathāṅgulimāla-vantaṁ;**

**Iddhībhi-saṅkhata-mano jitavā Munindo,**

**Taṁ tejasā bhavatu te jaya-maṅgalāni.**

*Aṅgulimāla---lifting up a sword in hand, extremely dreadful, running along the path for a distance of a league---the Lord of Muni (the Buddha), whose mind was equipped with psychic powers (iddhi), conquered him. By that power, may joyful victories be yours!*

**5. Katvāna kaṭṭha mudaraṁ iva gabbhinīyā,**

**Ciñcāya duṭṭha-vacanaṁ janakāya-majjhe;**

**Santena soma-vidhinā jitavā Munindo,**

**Taṁ tejasā bhavatu te jaya-maṅgalāni.**

*Having made a wooden block belly, like a pregnant woman, by Ciñcā a wicked word in the midst of the public assembly; the Lord of Muni (the Buddha) conquered that by peaceful and gentle way. By that power, may joyful victories be yours!*

**6. Saccaṁ vihāya mati-saccaka-vāda-ketuṁ,**

**Vādābhiropita-manaṁ ati-andha-bhūtaṁ;**

**Paññā-padīpa-jalito jitavā Munindo,**

**Taṁ tejasā bhavatu te jaya-maṅgalāni.**

*Having abandoned truth, the banner of Saccaka\'s argumentative doctrine---whose mind was puffed up with debate, having become excessively blind---the Lord of Muni (the Buddha), illuminated by the light of wisdom (paññā), conquered him. By that power, may joyful victories be yours!*

**7. Nando'pananda bhujagaṁ vibudhaṁ mahiddhiṁ,**

**Puttena thera-bhujagena damāpayanto;**

**Iddhūpadesa-vidhinā jitavā Munindo,**

**Taṁ tejasā bhavatu te jaya maṅgalāni.**

*Nando'pananda---the nāga, wise, great psychic power (mahā-iddhi)---by the son (Mahāmoggallāna), (transforming into) an elder nāga, causes him to be tamed; the Lord of Muni (the Buddha) conquered by way of psychic power instruction. By that power, may joyful victories be yours!*

**8. Duggāha-diṭṭhi-bhujagena sudaṭṭha-hatthaṁ,**

**Brahmaṁ Visuddhi-jutim-iddhi Bakābhidānaṁ;**

**Ñānāgadena-vidhinā jitavā Munindo,**

**Taṁ tejasā bhavatu te jaya maṅgalāni.**

*A brahmā entitled name Baka, who possessed of purity, radiance, and psychic power---whose hand was severely bitten by the serpent of tenaciously held wrong views; the Lord of Muni (the Buddha) conquered by the medicine of knowledge (ñāṇa). By that power, may joyful victories be yours!*

Commentary: **abhidānaṁ**, possession of a **name** is of **glory, honor, power, and high status** for brahmā deities.

**The Vedic Concept of *Nāman* (Name as Essence and Glory):** In the Ṛgveda, a name is intrinsically linked to **Rūpa** (form), **Tejas** (splendor), and **Mahiman** (greatness/glory).

**Name as Manifest Power**: In Ṛgveda (e.g., RV 10.71.1), the acts of creation and divine manifestation occur when hidden principles are given names: *saktum iva tita-ünā punanto yatra dhīrā manasā vācam akrata \|*

*\"When the wise fashioned speech through their mind\... they brought forth the sacred names.\"*

- **The Secret/Glorious Name (*Guhyaṁ Nāma*)**: Gods in the Ṛgveda possess hidden or sacred names (*guhyaṁ nāma*) that embody their supreme power and glory. To know or reveal a deity\'s name is to evoke their active, radiant presence (*tejas*).

- **Naming as Elevation**: An unnamed entity in Vedic ritual topology is unmanifest, chaotic, or non-existent (*asat*). To possess a distinct *nāman* established in the higher realms (*parama-pada*) is the ultimate mark of divine hierarchy and distinction.

**9. Etā'pi Buddha jaya-maṅgala-aṭṭha-gāthā, Yo vācako dina-dine sarate-m-atandi; Hitvān\'aneka-vividhāni c'upaddavāni,**

**Mokkhaṁ sukhaṁ adhi-gameyya naro sapañño.**

*Whoever---a reciter unwearied day by day---recollects these eight verses of the Buddha\'s victorious auspicious signs, having overcome many and various dangers, that wise person would attain the bliss of liberation (mokkha-sukha).*

**Bhavatu sabba maṅgalaṁ, rakkhantu sabba devata;**

**Sabba Buddhānubhāvena, sadā sotthi bhavantu te.**

*May there be complete auspiciousness, may all devas protect; by the power of all Buddhas, may well-being always be yours.*

**Bhavatu sabba maṅgalaṁ, rakkhantu sabba devata;**

**Sabba Dhammānubhāvena, sadā sotthi bhavantu te.**

*May there be complete auspiciousness, may all devas protect; by the power of all Dhamma, may well-being always be yours.*

**Bhavatu sabba maṅgalaṁ, rakkhantu sabba devata;**

**Sabba Saṅghānubhāvena, sadā sotthi bhavantu te.**

*May there be complete auspiciousness, may all devas protect; by the power of all Saṅgha, may well-being always be yours.*

**Sabbe Buddhā balappattā, paccekānañ ca yaṁ balaṁ;**

**Arahantānaṁ ca tejena, rakkhaṁ bandhāmi sabbaso.**

*All Buddhas who have attained supreme strength, and whatever power belongs to the Pacceka-buddhas (Solitary/Independently Awakened Ones), and by the spiritual majesty of the Arahants---I establish complete protection in every way!*

**Sabbe Buddhā balappattā, paccekānañ ca yaṁ balaṁ;**

**Arahantānaṁ ca tejena, rakkhaṁ bandhāmi sabbaso.**

*All Buddhas who have attained supreme strength, and whatever power belongs to the Pacceka-buddhas (Solitary/Independently Awakened Ones), and by the spiritual majesty of the Arahants---I establish complete protection in every way!*

**Sabbe Buddhā balappattā, paccekānañ ca yaṁ balaṁ;**

**Arahantānaṁ ca tejena, rakkhaṁ bandhāmi sabbaso.**

*All Buddhas who have attained supreme strength, and whatever power belongs to the Pacceka-buddhas (Solitary/Independently Awakened Ones), and by the spiritual majesty of the Arahants---I establish complete protection in every way!*

**Etena saccena suvatthi hotu!**

*By the power of this Truth, may there be safety and well-being!*

## Pañca Sila • Five Precepts

### Aradhana • Requesting

**Ahaṁ bhante, tisaraṇena saddhim, Pañcama silaṁ, dhammaṁ yācāmi, anuggahaṁ katvā sīlaṁ detha me, bhante.**

*Bhante, may I request the five precepts along with the three refuges. Having shown compassion, please grant me the precepts, Bhante.*

**Dutiyam\'pi,** **Ahaṁ bhante, tisaraṇena saddhim, Pañcama silaṁ, dhammaṁ yācāmi, anuggahaṁ katvā sīlaṁ detha me, bhante.**

*For the second time, may I request the five precepts along with the three refuges. Having shown compassion, please grant me the precepts, Bhante.*

**Tatiyam\'pi,** **Ahaṁ bhante, tisaraṇena saddhim, Pañcama silaṁ, dhammaṁ yācāmi, anuggahaṁ katvā sīlaṁ detha me, bhante.**

*For the third time, may I request the five precepts along with the three refuges. Having shown compassion, please grant me the precepts, Bhante.*

**\[Bhante\] Yam ahaṁ vadāmi, taṁ vadehi (vadetha)**

*Whatever I say, you (all) repeat after me.*

**\[Student\] Āma Bhante.**

*Yes, Bhante.*

### Tisarana • Three Refuges

**Namo Tassa Bhagavato Arahato Sammāsambuddhasa (X3)**

*Homage to the Blessed One, the Supreme Arahant, the Rightly and Fully Awakened One!*

**Buddhaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Dhammaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Saṁghaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

*I go to the Buddha, Dhamma and Sangha as my refuge.*

**Dutiyampi Buddhaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Dutiyampi Dhammaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Dutiyampi Saṁghaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

*For the second time, I go to the Buddha, Dhamma and Sangha as my refuge.*

**Tatiyampi Buddhaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Tatiyampi Dhammaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

**Tatiyampi Saṁghaṁ Saranaṁ Gacchāmi.**

*For the third time, I go to the Buddha, Dhamma and Sangha as my refuge.*

\[**Bhante**\] **Tisaraṇa Gamanaṁ Sam-Puṇṇaṁ.**

*Taking refuge is complete.*

**\[Student\] Āma Bhante.**

*Yes, Bhante.*

### Pañca sīla • Five Precepts

**1. Pāṇātipātā veramaṇī, sikkhāpadaṃ samādiyāmi**

*I undertake the training step to abstain from destroying the life force.*

**2. Adinnā-dānā veramanī, sikkhāpadaṃ samādiyāmi**

*I undertake the training step to abstain from taking what is not given.*

**3. Kamesu micchā-cārā veramanī, sikkhāpadaṃ samādiyāmi**

*I undertake the training step to abstain from sexual misconduct (wrong conduct in sexual pleasures).*

**4. Musā-vādā veramanī, sikkhāpadaṃ samādiyāmi**

*I undertake the training step to abstain from false speech.*

**5. Surā-meraya-majja-pamā-daṭṭhānā veramanī, sikkhāpadaṃ samādiyāmi**

*I undertake the training step to abstain from fermented drink, distilled liquor, and intoxicating substances---the ground of spiritual laxity.*

**\[Bhante\] Tisaranena saddhim Pañcama silaṁ dhammam sādhukam surakkhitam katvā Appamādena sampādethabbam.**

*Maintaining well the Five Precepts with the Three Refuges, strive on with diligence.*

**\[Student\] Sādhu, Bhante.**

*Yes, bhante*

### Patthanā & Punnānumodana • Aspirations & Sharing of Merits

**Idaṁ me puññam, āsavakkaya vahaṁ hotu,**

**Idaṃ me silam, nibbānassa paccayo hotu;**

**Mama puñña-bhāgaṁ, sabbasattānaṁ bhājemi,**

**Te sabbe me samaṁ, puñña-bhāgaṁ labhantu.**

*May this merit of mine be conducive to the destruction of the āsavas (defilements). May this virtue of mine be a (supportive) condition for Nibbāna. I share my merits done today with all beings. May all those beings get an equal share of the merits shared by me.*

### How Five Precepts Foster Personal Development

**Five Precepts (Pañcasīla): The Foundation for Ethical Integrity, Sense Restraint, and Spiritual Liberation**

These five training steps (*sikkhāpada*) are not external commands imposed from above, but active, conscious footholds taken voluntarily by the practitioner. They are foundational principles to be reflected upon and deeply integrated into one\'s daily life.

Serving as a precise guide for ethical conduct, they encourage both lay practitioners aspiring to be Arahanist renunciants to consider the immediate and karmic consequences of their bodily and verbal actions. By consciously striving to uphold these training steps, one embarks on a journey of internal purification, self-mastery, and spiritual liberation that fosters profound development across several key dimensions:

- **Moral Compass:** The training steps provide a clear, unambiguous compass, guiding individuals toward wholesome (*kusala*) actions and away from harmful ones. Upholding them establishes an unblemished foundation, bringing a sense of deep inner peace and integrity while entirely eliminating the agitation of guilt and remorse.

- **Self-Discipline and Self-Control:** Upholding these steps requires rigorous self-discipline and sense restraint (*indriya-saṃvara*). By choosing to abstain from harmful or unwholesome actions, practitioners strengthen their willpower, master impulse control, and develop the capacity for complete mental self-regulation.

- **Cultivation of Positive Qualities:** Each training step directly corresponds to a noble spiritual quality: non-violence (*ahiṃsā*), generosity and fearlessness, responsibility and respect for vital energy (*prāṇa*), absolute truthfulness, and unshakeable spiritual zeal (*appamāda*). By practicing these steps, one actively cultivates these virtues, leading to an ethically unassailable life.

- **Improved Relationships and Social Harmony:** Living ethically according to these steps fosters healthy, harmonious relationships with all living beings. Trust, mutual respect, and clear communication form the bedrock of strong communities, establishing safety and social stability.

- **Mental Clarity and Focus:** By completely abstaining from physical intoxicants and guarding against heedlessness (*pamāda*), practitioners maintain pristine mental clarity. This preserves the sharp focus required to navigate life\'s challenges with unwavering wisdom and awareness.

- **Foundation for Samādhi and Liberation:** The training steps provide the indispensable foundation for higher mental cultivation and deep *samādhi*. A mind burdened by ethical breaches or sensory agitation can never achieve deep stillness. By living in strict accordance with *sīla*, the practitioner creates the ideal inner environment for the *citta* to disenchant itself from worldly bondage and transcend.

Gautama, the supreme arahant, articulated a precise and ordered training progression for those who commit to the path of renunciation. This system unfolds in stages that cultivate ethical grounding, sense restraint, disciplined conduct, and increasing withdrawal from the world, preparing a practitioner for profound inner transformation.

The training path for lay practitioners emphasizes ethical conduct, sense restraint and mental discipline as integral to personal development, regardless of one\'s lifestyle. The laity precepts, a set of ethical guidelines, form a crucial foundation for this development, providing a framework for cultivating wholesome qualities and start to reduce sources of happiness from the worldly life and start to cultivate inner joy independent from changing external conditions, which is crucial for worldly renunciation when walking on the path toward Arahantship.

The most common set of precepts for lay Buddhists and Arahanists are the Five Precepts (*Pañca-sīla*), which serve as the bedrock of ethical life:

#### 1). The Practice of Non-Harming

**The Fundamental Rule**

**Pāṇātipātā veramaṇī sikkhāpadaṃ samādiyāmi**

*\"I undertake the training step to abstain from destroying and harming the life force.\"*

**(1). Etymological and Direct Meaning**

To understand the exact legal and ethical boundary of this training rule, one must look directly at its roots. The word **pāṇa** corresponds to the ancient concept of *prāṇa*---the vital breath, energy, and life force animating any living entity. The term **atipāta** is derived from *ati* (beyond, over) + *pat* (to fall), functioning in its causative sense as **\"causing to fall down completely,\" \"striking down,\"** or **\"slaying.\"**

Thus, **pāṇātipāta** denotes the **destruction, severance, or taking of the life force** of a sentient being. While harming or draining vitality violates the broader ethical principle of non-harming (*ahiṃsā*), this training rule sets its exact, unambiguous threshold at **atipāta**---refraining from the complete overthrow or destruction of that life force.

While common legalistic definitions often limit this precept to the final act of physical destruction, the root term **pāṇa** reveals a much broader scope. Any action that diminishes, exhausts, or violently disrupts the vital life force of another being directly impinges upon **pāṇa**. Thus, abstaining from **pāṇātipāta** is fundamentally an active commitment to **Ahiṃsā** (non-harming).

**(2). Non-Harming as the Core Principle**

This precept extends beyond simply not taking life force. It encourages the development of compassion and non-violence toward all living beings, fostering respect for life in all its forms. This cultivates kindness, reduces aggression, and promotes peace with the world.

When non-harming is established as the foundational orientation, the scope of practice naturally expands:
